Identify parameters not used in our submission: `expert_mask`, `doweight_stage1`, sorting options\n3. Check if `fused_moe` has config/tuning knobs (block sizes, number of stages, etc.)\n4. Search GPU MODE Discord or GitHub issues for MI355X MoE optimization tips\n5. Study the difference between our E=257 (shared expert) vs E=33 configs — our E=33 configs are proportionally faster\n\n**Definition of Done:**\n- [ ] List of tunable fused_moe parameters documented\n- [ ] Hypothesis for 2-3 optimization approaches\n\n**Verify:** Research findings documented in results.md or plan notes\n\n---\n\n### Task 2: Optimize MoE Kernel (target: top 20)\n\n**Objective:** Improve MoE from 185 us (rank 34/43) to under ~170 us (target top 20). The gap to leader is only 1.28x — parameter tuning may be sufficient.\n\n**Dependencies:** Task 1\n\n**Files:** Modify `kernels/moe-mxfp4/submission.py`\n\n**Optimization ideas (try in order):**\n1. **`doweight_stage1=True`** — applies topk_weights in stage 1 instead of stage 2, potentially reducing memory traffic\n2. **`expert_mask` parameter** — may enable skipping empty experts for better load balancing\n3. **Token sorting** — sort tokens by expert assignment before dispatch to improve memory coalescing\n4. **Shape-specific dispatch** — different parameters for E=257 vs E=33 configs\n5. **Pre-compute shared expert output** — the shared expert (index 256) processes ALL tokens with weight=1.0; computing it as dense GEMM + adding to routed MoE output could be faster\n\n**Workflow per attempt:**\n1. Modify submission.py\n2. `--mode test` (verify correctness, ~2 min)\n3. If passes: `--mode benchmark` (get timing)\n4. If faster: `--mode leaderboard` (official submission)\n5. Record result in results.md\n\n**Definition of Done:**\n- [ ] At least 3 parameter variations tested\n- [ ] Best result submitted to leaderboard\n- [ ] Results documented with timing per config\n\n**Verify:** `popcorn submit --mode leaderboard` with improved timing\n\n---\n\n### Task 3: Tune MLA NUM_KV_SPLITS per Batch Size\n\n**Objective:** The current MLA uses a fixed `NUM_KV_SPLITS=32` for all batch sizes. Smaller batch sizes (bs=4) likely need fewer splits for optimal parallelism, while larger batches (bs=256) might benefit from more.\n\n**Dependencies:** None\n\n**Files:** Modify `kernels/mixed-mla/submission.py`\n\n**Steps:**\n1. Add batch-size-dependent NUM_KV_SPLITS dispatch:\n   - bs=4: try NUM_KV_SPLITS={8, 16} (fewer splits = less reduction overhead)\n   - bs=32: try NUM_KV_SPLITS={16, 32}\n   - bs=64: try NUM_KV_SPLITS={32}\n   - bs=256: try NUM_KV_SPLITS={32, 64}\n2. Submit with `--mode benchmark` to find best per-config splits\n3. Implement the winning configuration as a lookup table\n\n**Definition of Done:**\n- [ ] Tested at least 2 NUM_KV_SPLITS values\n- [ ] Best configuration submitted to leaderboard\n- [ ] Timing improvement documented\n\n**Verify:** `popcorn submit --mode benchmark` shows improvement over 191 us baseline\n\n---\n\n### Task 4: Optimize MLA Kernel (target: top 25)\n\n**Objective:** Beyond NUM_KV_SPLITS tuning, explore deeper optimizations for MLA. Target: break into top 25 (~72 us at rank 15 cluster).\n\n**Dependencies:** Task 3\n\n**Files:** Modify `kernels/mixed-mla/submission.py`\n\n**Optimization ideas (try in order):**\n1. **MXFP4 KV cache** — use `kv_data[\"mxfp4\"]` instead of `kv_data[\"fp8\"]` for 2x bandwidth savings. The input already provides pre-quantized MXFP4 KV data.\n2. **`fast_mode=True`** — the metadata builder has a `fast_mode` option we set to False; try True\n3. **Non-persistent mode for small batches** — persistent mode has overhead; bs=4 might be faster without it\n4. **Tune `kv_granularity`** — currently `max(PAGE_SIZE, 16)`; try 32 or 64\n5. **Pre-allocate output buffer** — move allocation outside timed region if the harness allows\n\n**Definition of Done:**\n- [ ] At least 2 optimization approaches tested\n- [ ] Best result submitted to leaderboard\n- [ ] MLA rank improved from 40/54\n\n**Verify:** `popcorn submit --mode leaderboard` shows rank improvement\n\n---\n\n### Task 5: Fix GEMM JIT Call-Site Issue\n\n**Objective:** Stop delegating to `ref_kernel` (which adds Python overhead). Call `aiter.get_triton_quant` and `aiter.gemm_a4w4` directly from `submission.py` while maintaining correctness.\n\n**Dependencies:** None\n\n**Files:** Modify `kernels/mxfp4-mm/submission.py`\n\n**Known issue:** Calling `aiter.get_triton_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)` from `submission.py` produces different Triton JIT compilations than from `reference.py`, causing ~82% element mismatches at ~1-3% error.\n\n**Fix strategies:**\n1. **Force Triton cache invalidation** — set `TRITON_CACHE_DIR` env var or call `torch.cuda.empty_cache()` before quant\n2. **Use `aiter.get_torch_quant`** instead of `get_triton_quant` — torch-based quantization may be deterministic (reference.py uses it in `generate_input`)\n3. **Import quant function from reference** — `from reference import ref_kernel` works; try importing just the quant function\n4. **Call `get_triton_quant` with explicit kwargs** to match the exact JIT key\n\n**Definition of Done:**\n- [ ] `custom_kernel` calls gemm_a4w4 directly (no ref_kernel delegation)\n- [ ] Passes `--mode test` with 0.0 max error\n- [ ] Benchmark shows improvement over 24 us baseline\n\n**Verify:** `popcorn submit --mode test` passes; benchmark < 20 us\n\n---\n\n### Task 6: Optimize GEMM Kernel (target: top 40)\n\n**Objective:** Once the JIT issue is fixed, optimize the GEMM kernel. Target: match or beat reference baselines (8-20 us range).\n\n**Dependencies:** Task 5\n\n**Files:** Modify `kernels/mxfp4-mm/submission.py`\n\n**Optimization ideas:**\n1. **Shape-dependent routing** — small M (4, 16) vs large M (64, 256) may benefit from different kernel strategies\n2. **Triton GEMM for large M** — aiter has `aiter.ops.triton.gemm.basic.gemm_afp4wfp4` as alternative\n3. **Pre-shuffle optimization** — B is already pre-shuffled in input; verify we're using it efficiently\n4. **Quantization fusion** — try to overlap A quantization with B setup\n\n**Definition of Done:**\n- [ ] At least 2 optimization approaches tested\n- [ ] Best result submitted to leaderboard\n- [ ] GEMM rank improved from 67/68\n\n**Verify:** `popcorn submit --mode leaderboard` shows rank improvement\n\n## Popcorn CLI Reference\n\n```bash\nKERNELS=/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/.worktrees/spec-luma-amd-speedrun/research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/kernels\nCLI=~/.local/bin/popcorn-cli\n\n# Test → Benchmark → Leaderboard (always in this order)\n$CLI submit --no-tui --mode test --gpu MI355X --leaderboard amd-moe-mxfp4 $KERNELS/moe-mxfp4/submission.py\n$CLI submit --no-tui --mode benchmark --gpu MI355X --leaderboard amd-moe-mxfp4 $KERNELS/moe-mxfp4/submission.py\n$CLI submit --no-tui --mode leaderboard --gpu MI355X --leaderboard amd-moe-mxfp4 $KERNELS/moe-mxfp4/submission.py\n\n$CLI submit --no-tui --mode test --gpu MI355X --leaderboard amd-mixed-mla $KERNELS/mixed-mla/submission.py\n$CLI submit --no-tui --mode test --gpu MI355X --leaderboard amd-mxfp4-mm $KERNELS/mxfp4-mm/submission.py\n```\n\n**Confirmed leaderboard names:** `amd-mxfp4-mm`, `amd-mixed-mla`, `amd-moe-mxfp4`\n\n**No per-user rate limit.** Submit freely. Retry on transient artifact download failures.\n\n## Risks\n\n| Risk | Mitigation |\n|------|------------|\n| MoE gains are marginal (already near reference perf) | Focus on parameter tuning, not algorithmic changes. Even 5% helps. |\n| MLA top entries use custom ASM (unreachable from Python) | Target mid-pack (top 25), not top 3. MXFP4 KV is a realistic 2x win. |\n| GEMM JIT bug has no workaround | Try torch_quant path; worst case stay at ref_kernel delegation. |\n| Slow iteration (~2 min/submit) | Batch optimizations: test multiple params per submission when possible. |\n| Correctness regression from optimization | Always `--mode test` before `--mode benchmark`. |"

> AGENT

The plan has been completely rewritten. Here's a summary of what changed:

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
- **Old plan** was about getting initial submissions working (fix correctness, discover leaderboard names) — all completed.
- **New plan** is pure optimization with 6 tasks ordered by ROI: MoE research → MoE optimize → MLA tuning → MLA optimize → GEMM fix → GEMM optimize.
- **MoE is the top priority** because it's only 1.28x from leader — parameter tuning in `fused_moe` (like `doweight_stage1`, token sorting, shared expert separation) could close the gap.
- **MLA's biggest win** is switching from FP8 KV to MXFP4 KV (2x bandwidth savings — the input already provides pre-quantized MXFP4 data!) and tuning `NUM_KV_SPLITS` per batch size.
- **GEMM is blocked** by the Triton JIT call-site bug, but there's a potential fix: use `get_torch_quant` instead of `get_triton_quant`.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`
